資料中心監管風暴衝擊 AI 供應鏈，美股全面重挫
2026 年 11 月 06 日
一、 市場動態
1. 資料中心監管疑慮引爆 AI 供應鏈賣壓，美股選後漲勢一日反轉：民主黨取得參、眾兩
院多數，美國進入分裂政府格局。S&P 500 於 11 月 4 日先漲 1.58%，11 月 5 日卻重
挫 4.36%，創2025 年 4 月以來最大單日跌幅 ；Nasdaq 跌 5.52%，費半大跌 12.25%，
十一大產業全數收黑。主因有三：一是資料中心開發成為州級政策議題，德州州長當
選人主張暫停新建，全美新增容量約三成可能受影響；二是高通與 Arm 財報不如預

期；三是建設遞延將推高依賴外部融資業者的持有成本。費半重挫前已自 6 月高點回
落近三成，政策不確定性進一步放大跌幅（詳見表一）。
表一：美股主要指數表現（2026/11/05 收盤）
主要指數 收盤點位 單日漲跌幅
S&P 500 指數 7,353.73 −4.36%
Nasdaq 綜合指數 24,315.00 −5.52%
費城半導體指數 9,011.14 −12.25%
資料來源：Reuters、Nasdaq、元大銀行整理
2. 波動率指數急升，公債殖利率全面走低，油價反彈：11 月 5 日避險情緒升溫，VIX 盤
中升至 28.09（前日 16.61）。資金湧入美債，2 年、5 年、10 年及 30 年期殖利率分
別降至 4.46%、4.59%、4.84%及 5.25%，呈牛市陡峭化；12 月升息一碼機率由約
83%降至約 65%。布蘭特原油反彈 3.88%至每桶 98.29 美元，通膨前景仍具不確定性
（詳見表二） 。
表二：美債殖利率與避險資產變化（2026/11/05）
項目 11/5 數值 變動

2 年期公債殖利率 4.46% −12 bps
10 年期公債殖利率 4.84% −6 bps
VIX 波動率指數（盤中高點） 28.09 前日收盤 16.61
布蘭特原油期貨（美元／桶） 98.29 +3.88%
12 月升息一碼機率（CME） 約 65% 前日約 83%
資料來源：Bloomberg、WSJ／CME、元大銀行整理
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨橫掃國會及州長選舉，資料中心開發成為州級政策議題：德州州長當選人 Gina
Hinojosa 主張暫停新建資料中心，俄亥俄州與喬治亞州當選人也提出限制。地方政府

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
可透過電價分攤、建設許可及稅務優惠影響新案進度，UBS 估計 2028 年全美新增資
料中心容量約三成可能受影響。國會對科技巨頭與資料中心用電的監督亦可能增強，
半導體、電力設備及資料中心類股成為賣壓重心。
2. 高通手機晶片業績低於預期，加重半導體類股賣壓：高通財年第四季調整後 EPS 年減
28%至 2.17 美元，手機晶片營收年減 26%至 51.37 億美元，低於預期，主因蘋果相
關收入減少；汽車與物聯網營收雖年增 36%，仍難抵銷缺口，股價隔日收跌 9.5%，
進一步放大半導體跌幅。
3. 融資成本與投資回收壓力加重，依賴外部融資業者首當其衝：資料中心資本支出多發

生於上線前，若電力、許可或工程延後，收入認列隨之推遲，利息與持有成本卻須持
續負擔。儘管 12 月升息機率降至約 65%，借款成本仍高，依賴外部融資或單一客戶
合約的 AI 基礎建設業者將面臨回報壓縮與再融資風險。
4. 科技股在本次重挫前已明顯修正 ， 評價落至極低水準：費半自 6 月 22 日高點 14,634.72
點已回落逾 30%，Nasdaq 亦自 10 月 6 日高點 27,599.89 點回落逾 10%。市場質疑
AI 投資效益，降低對高評價企業的容忍度，政策不確定性因而放大修正。近期跌幅較
屬估值下調而非基本面惡化，若晶片訂單與獲利預估維持穩定，市場有望回溫。
表三：11/5 主要科技股及類股表現
標的 單日漲跌幅 重點說明
費城半導體指數 −12.25% 選後跌幅最深，AI 供應鏈評價重估
高通（Qualcomm） −9.5% 手機晶片營收年減 26%，低於預期
軟體類股 ETF（IGV） −4.48% 跌幅明顯小於半導體
資料來源：Reuters、Bloomberg、元大銀行整理

三、 市場看法
1. 短期評價修正而非長期趨勢反轉，AI 資本循環仍處擴張階段：我們視本波為多頭格局
中的健康修正。修正反映股東對龐大資本承諾要求更高風險補償，而非 AI 變現失速：
Google Cloud 第三季營收年增 103%、 營業利益年增209%，四大雲端業者資本支出
預估上修至約 1.16 兆美元，顯示企業看見更大市場而主動加碼（詳見表四）。此外，
政策衝擊集中於州與地方，聯邦仍支持資料中心擴建，選舉結果本質是要求用電成本
內部化，而非否定 AI 投資。
表四：AI 基本面持續擴張──關鍵指標一覽
指標 最新數據 解讀
Google Cloud 第三季營收 年增 103% AI 需求快速轉化為收入
Google Cloud 第三季營業利益 年增 209% 獲利能力未因擴張而惡化
四大雲端業者資本支出預估 約 8,732 億→1.16 兆美元 企業主動加碼，投資循環仍擴張
資料來源：元大投顧、Bloomberg；元大銀行整理

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。 
2. 留意五大反彈訊號，利空逐步消化後市場可望回穩：（一）州級電價與併網制度若於
2027 年上半年 建立 ，延後 專案可望 下半年重新加速 ，眾議院 以 417 比 3 通過
《Ratepayer Protection Act》亦顯示跨黨派共識；（二）雲端業者資本支出指引未下
修、未見取消訂單；（三）11 月 10 日 CPI 及 13 日 PPI 物價月增率回落；（四）財政
部維持附息債標售規模，資金回流市場；（五）VIX 回落且投資等級債利差穩定。訊號
出現後，市場有望回歸基本面。 
3. 政策先影響建設速度，AI 需求仍須以訂單驗證：州級限制首先改變的是資料中心選址
與交付時程，而非 AI 需求本身。雲端客戶若維持支出計畫，專案可調整順序或轉往他
州，惟設備交付遞延將壓抑供應商近季營收；聯邦立法限制則須經國會及總統否決權，
短期落地難度高。若主要客戶訂單穩定，本次修正較屬建設時程遞延的風險重定價。 
4. 新增算力面臨遞延，既有算力相對有利：高盛估計可能收緊開發限制的州約占美國規
劃中資料中心容量的 45%，其中德州約占 25%。新案放慢下，大型雲端業者既有算力
可望受惠於利用率與議價能力提升，資本支出延後亦減輕現金流壓力；仍待併網的專
案及高槓桿業者則面臨回收遞延，產業鏈分化將擴大。 
5. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：彭博統計 1950 年以
來，美國期中選舉後 12 個月 S&P 500 平均報酬約 15%，19 次全數收正，最高為 1954
年的逾 30%，最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。府會分治抑制政策大幅轉向為選後行情主
要支撐，惟後續表現仍須觀察基本面(詳見下圖)。

6. 企業獲利仍強勁成長，本次修正以估值與風險溢價重估為主：彭博 11 月 4 日統計，
S&P 500 已公布財報公司中 79.85%優於預期，資訊科技更達 92.45%，第三季 EPS
成長率分別為 29.0%及 67.8%（詳見表五）。財報為美股提供中期支撐，短期評價則
取決於 2027 年獲利預估能否承受建設遞延與融資成本壓力。 
表五：S&P 500 第三季財報統計（截至 2026/11/04） 
類別 已公布家數 優於預期家數 優於預期比率 第三季 EPS 成長率 
S&P 500 397 317 79.85% 29.0%

7. 最新經濟數據仍具韌性，聯準會利率路徑仍具變數：初領失業救濟金降至 18.9 萬人，
第三季勞動生產力季增年率 3.6%、單位勞動成本季減 0.7%，經濟仍具韌性。惟油價
逼近每桶 100 美元，若通膨高於預期，升息定價恐再升溫，壓抑科技股估值與長債價
格。
8. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：若 AI 企業持續擴大
資本支出與外部融資，償債能力將受關注。大型投資等級科技企業現金流穩定，較能

吸收壓力；高槓桿或依賴單一客戶的 AI 基礎建設業者則較易受利差上升衝擊。信用利
差若維持穩定，風險仍以股權評價調整為主；若同步擴大，則須警惕金融條件收緊。
四、 投資建議：
1. 短期修正不改 AI 長期趨勢 ， 切勿恐慌殺低錯殺優質標的：本次重挫源自選後政策不確
定性與評價重新定價，AI 需求與企業獲利尚未反轉，屬多頭中的短期波動。應避免恐
慌出清而錯殺優質標的；已持有者以續抱為主，加碼宜分批逢低，避免高槓桿操作。
2. 以股債平衡穿越短期波動，等待基本面重新主導市場：我們認為，本次修正屬短期政
策衝擊與評價調整，中長期科技股展望仍正面。在政策與通膨明朗前，建議將集中度
過高的科技股部位，適度轉向波動較低的平衡型或多重資產商品，搭配優質債券穩定
收益，並保留股票部位分批布局優質標的，待反彈訊號確認後再提高風險資產比重。
3. 股票部位聚焦優質、評價回落且前景較佳的產業：修正後部分優質企業評價已回到合
理區間，股票建議聚焦：一、握有長期電力合約、已通電園區的大型雲端業者；二、
明年展望佳的 AI 供應鏈，如 ASIC、光通訊、電力設備、半導體設備及資安；三、跌

幅較小的軟體及非科技優質股。高度仰賴新建資料中心與外部融資的業者，宜待環境
明朗再評估。
4. 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價
調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現
階段不建議延長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利
率敏感度與資產配置穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中
及再融資需求可控的發行人仍較為穩健，對AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合
約現金流與債務到期結構。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點：
• AI資料中心疑慮衝擊科技股: 民主黨在期中選舉獲勝, 多位民主黨州長重提AI建設禁令。市場擔心將影
響AI基建擴張, 參議員重提「超級AI禁令」更引發市場恐慌。
• AI熱潮面臨獲利考驗: AI算力需求仍強, 然市場進一步放大檢視「AI投資能創造多少收益」。AI基礎建
設受阻打擊AI供應鏈, 但資料中心回報率成長仍有利產業前景。
• 就業數據與美國經濟: 美國10月ISM製造業指數升至55.5創近4年新高, ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市
場預期。經濟表現強勁, 通膨仍具黏性, Fed下一步仍傾向漸進升息。
2. 後續觀察重點：
• 10月CPI(11/10)、PPI(11/13)、輝達財報(11/18)。
策略思維
1. 股市-類股走勢分化: 選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股分化, 可提高非科技股比重, 透過持股多元分散, 降低投資
組合波動。科技股可逢低分批布局, 但宜控制持股比例。
2. 債市-Fed仍傾向升息, 殖利率高檔震盪: 經濟走強, 通膨具黏性, Fed仍有升息壓力, 資料中心建設受阻降低

資金及舉債需求, 10年債殖利率回落, 仍維持震盪格局。
3. 匯市-美元高檔震盪: 美元指數近期隨美債殖利率回落, 但受升息預期影響而維持高檔震盪格局。

民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次 美股年末表現
資料來源: 270towin.com, 截至2026/11/4。 註: 統計1986年以來資料。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美國｜期中選舉結果出爐, 民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次
• 美國府會衝突與「否決先生」: 川普失去國會支持, 剩餘任期出現跛腳危機, 目前白宮已研擬「無國會施政」劇本,
可能透過行政命令與關稅延續政策, 川普更宣稱將動用總統否決權來阻擋不認同的法案。
• 民主黨的制衡與調查: 民主黨宣稱將透過國會調查川普家族與政府機構, 更可能全面封殺川普的司法與高層人事任
命。金融市場並不擔心分裂國會帶來的政治僵局, 這將會削弱川普極端政策或激進財政擴張的可能性, 反而降低市
場不確定因素。金融市場更加關注的是, 選舉結果將主導科技、醫療、能源等核心產業政策的重分配。

Cathay’s View
美國AI資料中心禁令
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/6。
• 隨著美國期中選舉出爐, 民主黨多位勝選候選人已經在當選演說中, 再度將AI、資料中心及大型科技公司的監管問
題搬上檯面, 包括俄亥俄州、維吉尼亞州議員及伊利諾州州長, 他們強調從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科
技巨頭決定。
• 整體而言, 川普政府加速發展AI的方向不變, 但地方政府對資料中心的土地、電力及環境審查可能更加嚴格, 根據
UBS先前預估, 2028年的資料中心擴建可能有30%受到衝擊, 高盛預估有45%的影響。
資料中心｜資料中心建置將受到一定的衝擊
州長／德州Gina
Hinojosa(政見)
1. 暫緩新建資料中心: 暫停新資料中心興建及核准, 直到州議會制定完善法規, 保障居民參與
地方開發決策的權利。
2. 取消企業補貼、落實成本自負：要求業者自行承擔電力、用水及電網建設成本, 取消大型
企業租稅優惠, 避免成本轉嫁給一般民眾。

州長／伊利諾州JB
Pritzker(感言) 強調伊利諾州已制定全美最嚴格的AI監管規範, 科技富豪不能凌駕法律。
州長／賓州Josh
Shapiro(感言) 強調不能讓大型科技公司制定影響未來的規則, 地方社區應有最終決定權。
參議員當選人／俄亥俄州
Sherrod Brown(感言) 「I do not work for data centers.」（我不是為資料中心業者工作的)。
參議員當選人／南卡羅來
納州Annie Andrews(感言)社區決策應由當地居民決定, 而非華府的億萬富豪或資料中心業者。
參議員／維吉尼亞州Mark
Warner(感言)
AI雖有創新與創造產業的機會, 但也有負面影響, 從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科技
巨頭決定。

2028年的資本支出可能受波及
註: 5大CSP業者指谷歌、微軟、Meta、亞馬遜及甲骨文。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/3。
• 在AI需求持續強勁之際 , 大型雲端服務供應商擴大資本支出已成市場共識 , 市場預估2026 年整體資本支出將達
8,000億美元, 2027年更有望正式突破1兆美元。然而, 隨著AI監管趨嚴, 加上部分州政府開始考慮暫緩資料中心興
建, 未來AI基礎建設的擴張速度可能受到影響。
• 短期而言, 預期2027年CSP資本支出仍具一定延續性, 但若地方政府陸續提高建置門檻, 2028年投資計畫可能面臨
較明顯的延遲或調整。後續應持續關注大型CSP的資料中心布局, 是否轉往政策較友善的州別, 或進一步擴大海外
投資, 這將是影響未來AI資本支出規模與成長速度的重要變數。

儘管10月FOMC按兵不動, 官員態度仍偏鷹派 經濟數據支撐高利預期,  抵銷近期買盤效應
註: Logan為今年投票委員, Barkin為2027年投票委員。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/31。
註: 2-4月受就業與消費數據走弱影響、7-9月受就業及製造業數據降溫拖累, 經濟驚奇
指數回落; 惟油價與通膨預期影響, 美債殖利率維持高檔。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/2。
• Fed官員延續鷹派: 除10月支持升息的兩名官員以外, 近期多位官員亦重申持續升息方向, 成員涵蓋今年及明年具
投票權的委員。Fed態度暗示的漸進式升息立場大致與期貨市場預估一致, 料未來1年仍具2~3碼升息空間。
• 經濟數據強勁: 美國第三季GDP、10月ISM、9月JOLTS等數據均優於市場預期, 以及PCE增速持續走高, 經濟驚奇
指數的反彈, 恐緩和近期科技股賣壓 、高利吸引力推動的債市買盤效應 。雖今年經濟驚奇指數與 10年債利率走勢

時有背離, 但油價高檔、通膨預期擔憂仍使利率走勢不易呈現趨勢性下行。
• 展望後市, 1)期中選舉後的中東局勢仍有高度地緣不確定性, 2)季度再融資計畫反映公債供給壓力, 3)預估2027年
AI信用債淨發行規模 恐持續擴張。在通膨、債務供給與資金需求 三重因素支撐下, 高利率中樞環境將為市場常態。

融資需求居高不下, 2027Q1升至8000億美元 新增發債需求更偏短天期
註: 採12個月滾動加總(12M rolling sum)淨發行, 近一年淨發行約2兆美元。
資料來源: Bloomberg, Sifma, 截至2026/9/30。
財政部｜美國2027年Q1借款需求攀升, 短債供給占比將逐步升高
註: 單位為億美元。
資料來源: 美國財政部預估。
• 最新季度借款預估顯示, Q3實際淨發債規模7420億美元, 大致符合8月預估值(7390億美元)。季末TGA餘額9840億
美元亦高於目標9500億美元, 導致Q4淨發債需求較前次預估下修100億美元; 惟考量期初現金餘額影響, 借款需求
實際較前次預估增加240億美元。2027年第一季借款規模預估8000億美元, 財政融資需求仍維持高檔。
• 財政部維持「Bills優先、Coupon穩定」的發債策略, 短期新增融資需求主要由國庫券吸收, 以避免中長天期公債
供給進一步增加。此舉有助控制發債成本, 但也意味市場對短端資金需求的依賴度持續提升; 目前Bills占未償還市

場性公債比重約22%(截至9/30), 高於TBAC(財政部借款諮詢委員會)建議的20%水準, 未來若借貸需求擴大, 市場
對流動性事件的尾端風險擔憂及再融資壓力的關注度將進一步提升。

第三季GDP年化季增3.4% 核心PCE年增率升高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 美國第三季GDP年化季增3.4%, 增速創一年來新高, 並高於市場預期3.2%及Q2的2.2%。除了個人消費強勁, 民間
投資成長顯著: 在AI投資熱潮帶動下, 設備支出對GDP貢獻創2011年以來新高, 為支撐經濟成長強勁的兩大因素。
• 9月PCE與核心PCE分別年增3.6%及3.1%。以核心通膨觀察, 除了房租價格走高之外, 扣除住房的服務指標「超級
核心」亦同步反彈: 能源價格走升帶動運輸服務, 醫療保健、金融保險增速降幅有限, 核心通膨仍具黏性。
• 美國經濟活動持續擴張, 消費與企業投資表現同步穩健, 展現經濟韌性; 與此同時, 通膨增速居高不下, 均將支持聯
準會維持偏緊縮的貨幣政策立場, 隱含未來進一步升息空間。

10月ADP新增8.6萬與前月持平, 服務業仍為主要支撐 JOLTS反映就業信心改善
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 10月ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市場預期(7.5萬人)。主要由服務業帶動 (貿易/運輸/公用事業+3.9萬、教育/醫療
+2.8萬、休閒餐旅+1.9萬), 但專業與商業服務(-2.7萬)及商品製造相關行業仍顯疲弱。
• 9月JOLTS職缺數+33.3萬至752.3萬, 職缺/失業比同步上升; 從新增比例觀察, 政府部門+10.4萬職缺(約1/3), 勞動
需求屬溫和回穩, 非私部門全面性爆發。細項方面, 自主離職數(轉職信心)反彈、裁員率減少, 均為正向就業訊號。
• 雖美國勞動市場自疫情後的高峰放緩, 但目前低裁員率 、穩定就業成長仍反映就業韌性。隨市場對勞動需求走弱
的擔憂淡化, Fed短期內亦無須過度關注就業下行風險, 政策焦點仍將著重在通膨進展。

英國物價與央行利率 MPC委員投票傾向
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/5。
英國BoE｜英央政策偏鷹調整, 市場上修升息次數
• 英國9月CPI年增率由8月3.1%升至3.5%, 能源與運輸價格仍是主要推手, 服務業通膨升至3.7%, 顯示物價壓力已不
再侷限於能源項目, 而有逐步擴散至內需部門的跡象。雖然失業率維持4.7%, 且職缺數持續下降, 但私人部門薪資
增速回升至3.0%, 10月綜合PMI升至53.1, 代表現行利率水準對需求抑制效果可能不如預期。
• 因此, 11月MPC投票結果由先前 6:3支持按兵不動 , 轉變為全面升息 。同步上修 2026、27年經濟成長至 1.6%和
1.3%。大幅上調通膨預期 , 預估將在2027年年中攀升至5%左右(不利情境下甚至可能升至 6.2%)。導致今年年底

升息機率提高至 50%附近, 2027年2月升息一次的機率幾乎底定 , 政策基調已由 「觀察能源衝擊是否短暫 」轉向
「預防通膨預期失控」。

英國公共部門淨借款占GDP比重預測 長天期利率
資料來源: OBR預估, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/3。
英國預算｜財政赤字上修與公債壓力仍在
• 英國最新秋季預算案延續 「財政紀律」主軸, 聚焦於降低家庭生活成本與支持企業投資 。主要措施包括向約2000
萬戶家庭提供一次性 £100能源補貼、將家用電力增值稅降至 0%的措施延長至2027年7月, 以及延長每公升5便士
的燃油稅減免與燃油稅凍結政策 。同時擴大服務業減免範圍 、提高酒吧與音樂場館商業稅減免 , 並將個人營業收
入免稅額由1000提高至3000英鎊, 銀行附加稅自3%提高至6%, 並加強稅務稽核以增加收入來源。

• 儘管新增支出相對克制, 英國財政前景仍較3月預算案略為惡化。預測顯示未來數年財政整頓速度放慢 , 對市場而
言, 這代表未來英國公債(Gilts)供給將維持高檔, 長端殖利率易保持在較高水準。

RBA調低成長率, 調高失業率與通膨預期 市場預期澳洲升息循環將更快見頂
澳洲｜澳洲央行如預期升息一碼, 但升息循環告終可能性大幅提高
• 繼9月按兵不動後 , 澳洲央行11月利率決議如市場預期升息一碼至 4.85%, 但同時出現3票反對, 同時聲明上刪除
「必要時進一步升息」的字眼, 市場解讀為「鷹派升息、鴿派暗示」, 本次升息循環可能接近終點。
• 總裁Bullock表示, 他們對成長、房市、勞動市場的下行風險權重高於通膨上行風險, 並主張考量已累積的升息與政
策時滯, 應等待更多資訊 。顯示RBA在打擊通膨的同時, 仍不得不向疲軟的經濟妥協 。考量升息循環已接近終點 ,
對澳幣的看法轉趨中立, 但對於澳債仍正向看待。
資料來源: RBA預估, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。

費半跌破7月低點, 健護指數衝高 美債殖利率回落, 地產指數反彈
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美股｜選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股走勢分化
• 民主黨拿下參、眾兩院過半席次, 並在地方選舉大勝, 政策預期變化加劇美股類股分化。資料中心禁建風潮恐拉長
申請審核流程, 遞延AI伺服器訂單, 半導體股持續修正, 費半指數跌破7月低點; 相較之下, 市場預期川普政府激進的
醫療削減政策將受到制衡, 使得健護產業成為選後主要的政策受惠者, 資金明顯轉進。
• 另一方面, 若資料中心建設放緩, 對資金及發債的需求降低, 美債殖利率應聲回落, 緩解利率敏感型產業的評價壓力,
地產指數明顯反彈。市場資金由高估值科技股轉向政策及利率受惠族群, 短期類股輪動加快, 美股多元配置重要性
進一步提升。

AI需求強勁, 帶動AMD資料中心翻倍成長 AMD毛利率續揚, 顯示需求依舊緊俏
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
• AMD第三季營收達133.8億美元, 年增45%, 高於市場預期, 非GAAP EPS達2.09美元, 年增74%, 同樣優於預期。
其中, 資料中心部門營收達85.5億美元, 年增97%, 創下歷史新高。此外, 毛利率達56%, 年增2個百分點, 營業利益
率則提升至29%, 反映AI業務規模擴大正帶動獲利能力改善。
• 展望方面, AMD對第四季及2027年營運前景展現高度信心。公司預估第四季營收可達170億美元, 年增約66%, 不
僅高於市場預期, 也意味營收成長動能將進一步加速。執行長蘇姿丰亦看好2027年營收成長可望超過80%, 主要來
自AI GPU出貨擴大, 以及伺服器CPU需求持續成長。不過, 遊戲業務營收年減40%, 顯示非AI市場仍存在壓力。

外資在韓股拋售力道仍大 韓股與韓元近期出現罕見的股匯反向
韓股｜近期外資拋售力道不減, 但韓股與韓元出現罕見股匯反向
• 外資對韓股的拋售未歇, 且此次企業回購結束, 本土法人與散戶買盤力道有限, 加上近期市場對於AI類股的疑慮加
深。基本面敘事改變與籌碼壓力下使韓股持續弱勢震盪。
• 另一方面, 韓股與韓元出現罕見的股匯反向, 在大漲/大跌的波動日尤其明顯, 與台股常見的外資賣、股市跌、台幣
貶的典型路徑相反。研判原因為(1)今年國際化改革後, 外資可直接於在岸市場交易韓元, 對本土匯市影響更直接。
(2)目前韓股外資的邊際流量以短線基金的快錢為主, 買/賣股同時搭配匯率避險/解除避險。整體而言, 韓股能否重
拾漲勢仍端看市場何時對AI產業的疑慮緩解。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。

Cathay’s View
五中全會要點 官方救市工具
中國｜著眼換屆, 五中全會先為未來一年定調; 人行等金融部門則聯手公布穩市政策
• 中共五中全會聚焦「持之以恒推進全面從嚴治黨」, 強調黨性修養、從嚴治吏、正風肅紀與反腐敗常態化, 並完成
重要人事調整與紀律確認。政治安全與權力集中置於優先位置, 這使得政策環境更偏向可控, 而非積極擴張。在考
核與問責壓力下, 官員避險心態可能延續, 進而影響經濟政策的執行力度與市場預期的修復速度。
• 與此同時, 人行會同國家金融監督管理總局、中國證監會三部門聯手開會, 重點有二: 1)將股票回購再貸款利率下
調一碼至1%, 以及2)第三批證券基金保險互換便利(Securities, Funds and Insurance companies Swap Facility,
SFISF)互換便利達1000億元申請(近前兩次合計), 以引導更多資金進入股市, 回應市場對「穩市」的期待, 有助提
振信心。
資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。

大盤企業獲利增長仍舊疲弱 A股指數與評價走勢
中國｜央媽祭出政策嘗試托底, 但大規模增量政策暫且無望, 加上國際氣氛影響, 指數需時間築底
• 在五中全會的政策指導下, 政治穩定先於經濟發展, 短期內難見大規模增量政策。考量現階段, 社會融資的年增速
仍偏緩慢, 顯示實體經濟融資需求與信用擴張動能尚未明顯回升。大盤企業整體獲利年增速依舊偏弱, 獲利修復基
礎不夠牢固。這進一步限制了市場上行動能, 加上近期國際股市氣氛不佳, 也使得估值修復難以一蹴而就。
• 考量數據與治理取向, 1)短線行情偏淡、指數需時間築底, 因為大規模刺激期待落空, 加上國際氣氛讓AI硬體股跟
跌, 長期均線呈現空頭排列下, 大盤轉為防禦與避險主導; 2)中長線在黨建先於發展的邏輯下, 資金還是只能流向國
企色彩高的類股, 加以人行將股票回購再貸款利率再度下調, 這應能為具有股息誘因的金融股形成支撐, 是大盤守

勢下, 非科技股的另一選項。
資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部維持台達電買進投資建議，主係：(1) 3Q26受缺料影響出貨
遞延，並認列存貨跌價損失，但漲價效益將自4Q26起逐步反映，延
續至1Q27，毛利率可望逐步回升，營運最壞情況已過；(2)資料中
心相關營收占比已達五成，液冷今年占比約13%，全年營收可望突
破900億元，明年仍維持成長；(3) 缺電與缺料雖然影響資料中心
建置節奏，但管理層表示訂單能見度仍高，主要是出貨時點遞延，
需求並未消失；(4) 股價回檔幅度已大，評價已明顯回落。綜上所
述，研究部微幅下修27年營收1.04兆元(原估1.05兆元)，EPS下修
至60.37元(原估62.68元)。評價方面，考量HVDC滲透率仍不明朗，
僅給予31倍本益比，目標價由2,400元下修至1,870元。整體而言，
營運最壞情況已過且股價回檔已多，維持買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26受缺料干擾，毛利率承壓但OPM大致持穩 
3Q26營收1,971億元(原估2,102億元)， 季增7.5%， 年增31.1%，
營收達成率93.7%，低於預期，主因是資料中心客戶長短料、
建置延遲，造成出貨遞延。毛利率降至34.1%(原估35.4%)，季
減1.6pts， 低於預期 ， 主係因出貨遞延公司因此認列約39億元
存貨跌價損失(約影響1.7%，涉及產品非VR相關產品，研究部
推測為ASIC產品)，加上漲價在3Q26幾乎還沒反映。不過費用
控管得宜，營利率16.6%(原估16.9%)，僅季減0.1pts，本業獲
利能力大致持穩 。 業外收益受股市評價影響 ， 由2Q26的44億元
降至10億元，EPS為9.04元(原估10.54元)，低於預期。

2. 4Q26毛利率可望止跌回升，營運低點已過 
由於缺料尚未完全解除，研究部微幅調整4Q26營收至2,248億
元(原估2,253億元)，但季增14.1%、年增39.1%，成長動能仍
強。獲利方面，公司表示4Q毛利率優於3Q，主係存貨跌價損失
新增認列有望減少 、 開始有較多新價格體現， 以及電源業務成
長 帶 動 產 品 組 合 改 善 ， 故 研 究 部 預 估 毛 利 率34.8%( 原估
35.8%)， 季增0.7pts； 營利率17.2%(原估18.2%)， 季增0.6pts；
EPS下修至10.14元(原估11.03元)。展望1Q27，研究部預估營
收2,308億元，季增2.6%，表現優於以往的淡季季節性，主要
是部分AI遞延訂單可望延續至1Q27出貨。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. 資料中心營收占比過半，訂單能見度仍高 
管理層表示 ， 今年資料中心相關營收已占總營收一半 ，冷卻系
統約占13%，液冷3Q營收約250億元，26年營收可望突破900億
元 ， 且公司對1H27液冷需求看法仍偏樂觀 。雖然缺電影響部分
資料中心的建置進度 ，但管理層強調訂單能見度高， 變化主要
是出貨時點的延後，並非需求消失。此外，研究部認為，台達
電在供應鏈管理上具備優勢， 隨著缺料逐漸緩解 ，遞延的需求
可望陸續轉為營收。

4. 然Rubin Ultra機櫃出現低壓方案，HVDC滲透率仍待觀察 
HVDC方面 ， 正負400V進展較快 ， 已開始出貨 ；800V則由NV主推 ，
進度較慢。根據供應鏈調查，Ultra平台目前已有低壓設計方
案，若TDP增幅不大，HVDC的省電效益相對有限，客戶未必非
用800V不可，因此研究部下修2027年營收預估。此外，公司已
通過馬來西亞檳城新據點的投資案 ， 為中長期AI電源需求預先
擴充產能。在Vicor相關訴訟上，仍在ITC調查程序中，短期對
出貨不會有影響，公司對自身IP技術具有信心。

5. 評價已回落，維持買進 
綜上所述，研究部下修2026年營收至7,645億元(原估7,781億
元)，年增37.8%；毛利率35.3%(原估35.9%)；EPS下修至36.77
元(原估39.16元)。 同步下修2027年營收至1.04兆元(原估1.05
兆元)， 年增36.4%； 毛利率36.1%(原估36.5%)；EPS下修至60.37
元(原估62.68元)，年增64.2%。考量HVDC滲透率仍不明朗，僅
給予31倍本益比， 目標價由2,400元下修至1,870元 。 惟股價評
價已明顯回落，且營運最壞情況已過，維持買進建議。

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上

VPEC (2455.TW): Optoelectronics mix upgrade driving GM expansion; 
3Q26 in line; Buy
2 November 2026 | 5:30AM HKT
3Q26 revenues increased 39% QoQ, with optoelectronics (mainly InP epiwafer for PD 
and CW lasers) revenues contribution up to 43% (vs. 27% in 2Q26), driving GM up to 
45% (vs. 39% in 2Q26). Along with opex ratio further improved to 13.7% (vs. 15.4% 
in 2Q26), VPEC’s OP income increased 87% QoQ in 3Q26, or 2% behind / 7% above 
our / Bloomberg consensus estimates. Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26E revenues to 
further increase by 14% QoQ, with GM up to 46% driven by product mix upgrade 
toward Optoelectronics. The company also announced its issuance of unsecured 
convertible bonds valued at NT$1.5bn (US$46m), to supplement its procurement in 
raw materials and equipment, reflecting the strong demand and VPEC’s commitment 
in capacity expansion; as we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates) where 
our US Internet team forecast that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in 
aggregate would grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026E-28E 
and where we raise our estimates by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E. We remain positive view on VPEC, which we believe is positioned to benefit 
from the AI server racks ramp up, optical networking specification upgrades across 
transmitters / CW lasers (toward 1.6T optical modules) and receivers (toward 200G 
PD), product expansion toward EML base epiwafers, further customer penetration 
for CW lasers, improving InP substrate supply, and opportunities in InP epiwafers for 
high power lasers for NPO and CPO. Maintain Buy.

YoY in 3Q26; Looking ahead, we expect +14% QoQ in 4Q26E, riding on new AI server 
racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade toward 1.6T optical

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price from 2029E P/E discounted back 
to 2027E at 10.5% COE (no change), and derive our target P/E multiple from refreshed 
peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year fundamentals (NI YoY and OPM). Based on our 
2029E-30E NI YoY of 18% (unchanged) and 2029-30E OPM of 38% (unchanged), and 
peers’ PEG&M of 0.4x (unchanged), we derive target P/E multiple at 22.0x (vs. 22.2x 
previously), reflecting a slight peer group multiple compression amid the product 
transition window from 800G to 1.6T optical transceivers, balanced by sustained 
long-term AI optical networking demand. Our new 12-month target price is at NT$785 
(vs. NT$793 previously). Our new TP implies 2027E P/E at 68.7x (vs. 69.4x previously), 
within the company’s trading range (vs. peak at 74x, and +1stv. at 47x). Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

### Page 3

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
05 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue are now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to capitalized on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

following a 1.3% decline in September

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia, fell
1.2% in October with slight revisions to previous months
again shaving a further 0.3% off prices. The correction
has now seen prices decline 6.7% from their March peak

with annual growth firmly in negative at –2.8%yr. Turnover
continues fall as well, down 22.5%yr. Housing-related
tax policy changes, interest rate rises and heightened
uncertainty have triggered a sharp contraction in markets.
The downturn is now comparable to the previous two
housing market corrections. Nationally, the downturn in
2022-23 saw prices decline 7.5% and turnover fall 29% peak
to trough. The 2017-18 correction saw prices fall 8.6% and

turnover by 24%, with larger falls in Sydney and Melbourne.
Note that unless specified, figures in titles, tables and
commentary are in non‑seasonally adjusted terms, consistent
with the ‘headline’ figures reported by Cotality. However,
figures shown in charts are in seasonally adjusted terms.
Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 5.6%qtr in October, previous estimates again revised
lower. The monthly pattern suggests declines re ‑accelerated

through September‑October after stabilising somewhat in July ‑
August. The RBA's interest rate rise in late September and a
further expected tightening likely contributed to the shift.
The detail shows Brisbane and Perth are now leading the
way in terms of monthly price declines although corrections
to date remain significantly larger in Sydney and Melbourne
while smaller capitals and regional areas continue to see much
milder downturns. Across price tiers, all segments are now

showing similar rates of decline after top price tiers had seen
a more pronounced and earlier downturn. 'Top tier' segments
have tended to lead recent price cycles.
Looking across jurisdictions,
• Sydney prices dropped 1.6% in October having averaged
similar‑sized declines over the previous five months. While
all segments and tiers continue to record falls, the last few
months has seen the pace of decline moderate a touch in the
top tier segments that have led cycles in the past, although

they are still a long way from stabilisation.
• Melbourne prices declined 0.8%mth, in line with
September's move and continuing the slower pace of
retreat. As noted, this likely reflects the weaker starting
point coming into the correction, prices already at 5½ year
lows. Interestingly, the detail shows the moderation in price
declines in recent months has been more pronounced for
'top tier' units where prices are back where they were in
2016.
Housing downturn deepens

3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
• Brisbane prices are tracking a very fast rate of decline
that is also seeing large downward revisions. Prices fall
2% in October with September and August now both
showing 2.1% falls, revised down from 1.5% and 1.6%

respectively (August having previously been estimated at
just 1%, July's move having seen a similar series of revisions
–1.2% to –1.8%). The combined effect has taken the rolling
3mth change to –6.1%qtr from –4.7%qtr last months and
–2.7%qtr in August and –0.6%qtr in July. At 0.4%yr, annual
price growth has virtually stalled flat having run at 18%yr as
recently as May. All tiers and segments are recording similar
rates of decline.
• Perth is seeing something very similar, prices dropping a

further 1.7% in October, with September's decline revised
down to 2.1% from –1.2% and August's to 2.0% from –1.7%.
At 3.9%yr, annual growth is still positive but off steeply
from the 24% pace back in May. As with Brisbane, all tiers
and segments are recording similar rates of decline.
• Adelaide dwelling prices posted a somewhat milder 1.2%
decline in October, , September's –1.3% decline marked
down to –1.7%. Annual price growth slowed to 3.7%yr,

similar to Perth's but with less of a whiplash from strong
gains to large declines. Turnover has also been much
steadier, sales holding flat over the last three months and
down just 12.4%yr.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.6%mth, 9.7%yr), relatively resilient
in Hobart (+0.5%mth, 5.5%yr) but weaker in Canberra
(–0.7%mth, –3.7%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.6%). Annual

prices growth is in the 1.4 ‑3.5%yr range.
All up, the October updates make for bleak reading. With the
RBA widely expected to raise interest rates again, the near
term outlook remains very challenging. Next week's consumer
sentiment update will show how this is shaping housing ‑
related sentiment – so far, consumer price expectations have
weakened but stopped short of swinging to the clear negative
consensus that emerged in the 2022 ‑23 correction. That now

looks to be at risk. Either way, momentum looks set to remain
weak into year‑end and, barring a significantly improved
inflation and interest rate outlook, well into 2027.

TUẦN 12-16/10 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ 
1.710 điểm và số mã còn giữ xu hướng tăng từ đáy tháng 7 suy gi ảm khiến xu 
hướng ngắn hạn trở nên trung tính.

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

### Page 4

4

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích

- Sau khi phủ sóng các thành phố lớn, chuỗi Long Châu đang mở rộng sang các vùng cấp 2, 3.

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

Document 321: Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-11-09).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

HOUSEHOLD SPENDING
INDICATOR BULLETIN
Spending resilient despite headwinds
4 November 2026
Key points
• Household spending rose 0.6%mth in
September, returning to growth after
August’s brief decline. Q3 spending increased
2.1%qtr, while volumes rose a more subdued

0.7%qtr, pointing to a sharp acceleration
in price growth through the quarter.
• Services spending rebounded and goods
spending remained supportive. Non-
discretionary spending outperformed
discretionary categories, with higher fuel
spending providing significant support.
• In real terms, spending growth over the
quarter was led by transport but with
most other categories also recording
gains. All states except Tasmania
recorded positive volume growth

in Q3, led by WA and Victoria.
• Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in
line with expectations and consistent with
our consumption outlook ahead of the Q3
National Accounts (due 2 December).
Q3 real household
spending:
0.7%qtr, 3.2%yr

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The monthly household spending indicator rose 0.6%mth in
September (nominal), exceeding both Westpac and market
expectations. We had anticipated a 0.5%mth contraction
(on the basis of a softer read on vehicle sales in the month),

while the market had pencilled in a 0.2%mth rise. Today’s
result instead marks a return to growth following August’s
brief contraction (revised down 0.1ppt to –0.1%mth), with
annual growth strengthening to 6.8%yr. For Q3 as a whole,
nominal spending rose 2.1%qtr, the strongest quarterly
result since the December quarter of 2022. In real terms,
volumes increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, pointing to a
sharp acceleration in price growth through the quarter.

Non-discretionary spending outperforms
By broad category, services spending more than reversed
last month’s decline, rising a solid 1.1%mth (+6.8%yr). Goods
spending held around its recent pace, increasing 0.3%mth
(+6.7%yr). Discretionary spending returned to growth in
September, rising 0.3%mth (+6.9%yr), although this failed to
fully unwind August’s decline.
The relative strength in non-discretionary spending observed
last month continued into September, with the category rising

1.2%mth (+6.5%yr). While the headline result was stronger,
the breakdown aligns with findings from our Westpac–DataX
Card Tracker, and Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel  which
show discretionary goods continuing to underperform other
categories (see here and here).
At the detailed level, transport and health led the gains,
both rising 1.5%mth. The increase in transport spending was
largely attributable to higher fuel spending, up 5.4%mth,
reflecting elevated fuel prices, with volumes down 2.2%mth.

The ABS also noted higher spending on vehicle repairs
and maintenance, while domestic air travel increased as
households travelled to attend major sporting events.
Recreation & culture rose 0.8%mth, while miscellaneous
goods & services (+0.6%mth), hotels, cafes & restaurants
(+0.5%mth), and clothing & footwear (+0.1%mth) also
recorded gains.
Partially offsetting these increases, food spending declined
0.1%mth, while furnishings & household equipment fell

0.2%mth, likely reflecting the ongoing weakness in housing
turnover (see here). Alcoholic beverages & tobacco recorded
the largest decline, falling 0.7%mth.
State outcomes were positive across the board in September.
WA led the gains, rising 1.1%mth and reversing last
month’s decline. Victoria also rebounded (+1.0%mth),
while Queensland (+0.6%mth), SA (+0.6%mth), Tasmania
(+0.5%mth) and NSW (+0.3%mth) all recorded increases.
Spending returns to growth in September

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Volume growth remains firm 
Nominal spending rose a firm 2.1%qtr in Q3, while volumes 
increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, matching the pace recorded 
in Q2. This implies a sharp acceleration in price growth to 
1.4%qtr, from 0.6%qtr in Q2, and marks the strongest quarterly 
increase in prices since the December quarter of 2022. Through 
the year, volume growth strengthened to 3.2%yr, remaining 
well above the 1.8%yr average recorded through 2025.
Transport led gains in volume terms, rising 1.6%qtr, 
likely partly reflecting base effects in air travel following 
disruptions associated with the Middle East conflict in the June 
quarter. Clothing & footwear (+1.2%qtr), health (+1.1%qtr), 
miscellaneous goods & services (+1.1%qtr), food (+0.5%qtr), 
recreation & culture (+0.5%qtr), and furnishings & household 
equipment (+0.1%qtr) also recorded increases. Hotels, cafes 
& restaurants spending declined 0.1%qtr in volume terms, 
while alcoholic beverages & tobacco was the main drag, falling 
1.2%qtr.
Most states recorded real spending growth over the quarter, 
led by WA (+1.4%qtr), Victoria (+0.9%qtr), NSW (+0.5%qtr), 
Queensland (+0.5%qtr) and SA (+0.4%qtr). Tasmania lagged its 
state peers, recording a 0.1%qtr decline.
Conclusion
The household spending indicator provides a useful guide to 
around two-thirds of total consumer expenditure. Importantly, 
it excludes several major categories included in the National 
Accounts measure of real household consumption, including 
rents and utilities.

Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in line with our 
expectations, with Westpac anticipating around a 0.6%qtr rise 
in Q3, and remains consistent with our view on consumption 
ahead of the Q3 National Accounts (due 2 December). 
However, this will likely be the high water mark for spending. 
The renewed deterioration in global energy markets and the 
cumulative effect of four rate increases over the first three 
quarters of the year are likely to weigh more heaveily on 
household spending in Q4.

Looking further ahead, households will continue to face 
a restrictive monetary policy environment, with the RBA 
delivering an additional rate increase at yesterday’s November 
meeting (see here). Combined with persistent consumer 
pessimism, ongoing cost-of-living pressures (see here) and a 
housing downturn impacting household wealth, spending looks 
set to slow further in 2027.

Document 429: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-09)

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA 
Chief Investment Officer, North Asia

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels

Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing 
AI concentration risk through a Total

Hannah Chew

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk , and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem.  
Non-AI-related factors , including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk . This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50%  to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses , although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI -related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversific ation. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long -term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives,  and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.
